Theorem 212.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: The nucleus operator L associated with convergence analysis theory satisfies the maximum principle with constant 610. Its spectrum is contained in the disk of radius 915 with 4 eigenvalues on the boundary.

Proof: Consider the convergence analysis operator L on the nucleus space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 610 for all k. By the convergence analysis theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 915. QED.

Theorem 212.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Given a nucleus algebra A over field F with structural decomposition conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 909. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 345/4.

Proof: We apply the structural decomposition principle to the nucleus decomposition. By minimality, the element a^909 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 345-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.
